primary concerns\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- **Authorization**: Written penetration testing agreement for the target\n- **Burp Suite Professional**: With Authorize extension for automated access control testing\n- **Multiple test accounts**: Accounts at each role level (admin, manager, user, guest)\n- **Application role matrix**: Documentation of what each role should and should not access\n- **curl/httpie**: For manual endpoint testing with different authentication contexts\n- **ffuf**: For discovering hidden endpoints that may lack access controls\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Map All Endpoints and Create Access Control Matrix\n\nDocument every endpoint and the expected access level for each role.\n\n```bash\n# Extract all endpoints from Burp Site Map\n# Target > Site Map > Right-click > Copy URLs in this host\n\n# Build a matrix of endpoints vs roles:\n# | Endpoint              | Admin | Manager | User | Guest |\n# |-----------------------|-------|---------|------|-------|\n# | GET /admin/dashboard  | Allow | Deny    | Deny | Deny  |\n# | GET /api/users        | Allow | Allow   | Deny | Deny  |\n# | PUT /api/users/{id}   | Allow | Deny    | Own  | Deny  |\n# | DELETE /api/posts/{id} | Allow | Allow   | Own  | Deny  |\n\n# Discover hidden endpoints\nffuf -u \"https://target.example.com/FUZZ\" \\\n  -w /usr/share/seclists/Discovery/Web-Content/raft-medium-directories.txt \\\n  -mc 200,301,302,403 -fc 404 \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $USER_TOKEN\" \\\n  -o endpoints.json -of json\n\n# API endpoint discovery\nffuf -u \"https://target.example.com/api/v1/FUZZ\" \\\n  -w /usr/share/seclists/Discovery/Web-Content/api/api-endpoints.txt \\\n  -mc 200,201,204,301,302,401,403,405 -fc 404 \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer $USER_TOKEN\"\n```\n\n### Step 2: Configure Automated Access Control Testing\n\nSet up Burp Authorize extension for parallel role-based testing.\n\n```\n# Install Authorize extension:\n# Burp > Extender > BApp Store > Search \"Authorize\" > Install\n\n# Configuration for three-tier testing:\n# 1. Browse the application as Admin (capture all requests)\n# 2. In Authorize tab:\n#    a. Add Regular User's session token in \"Replace cookies/headers\"\n#    b. Optionally add a second row for Unauthenticated (no auth header)\n\n# Example header replacement setup:\n# Row 1 (Low-privilege user):\n#   Cookie: session=low_priv_user_session\n#   Authorization: Bearer low_priv_token\n#\n# Row 2 (Unauthenticated):\n#   [Empty - removes all auth headers]\n\n# Enable interception in Authorize:\n# - Check \"Intercept requests from Proxy\"\n# - Check \"Intercept requests from Repeater\"\n\n# Authorize shows results as:\n# Green  = Properly restricted (different response for different user)\n# Red    = POTENTIALLY VULNERABLE (same response regardless of role)\n# Orange = Uncertain (needs manual verification)\n```\n\n### Step 3: Test Vertical Privilege Escalation\n\nAttempt to access higher-privilege functionality with lower-privilege accounts.\n\n```bash\n# Collect tokens for each role\nADMIN_TOKEN=\"Bearer admin_jwt_here\"\nMANAGER_TOKEN=\"Bearer manager_jwt_here\"\nUSER_TOKEN=\"Bearer user_jwt_here\"\n\n# Test admin endpoints with user token\nADMIN_ENDPOINTS=(\n  \"GET /admin/dashboard\"\n  \"GET /admin/users\"\n  \"POST /admin/users/create\"\n  \"PUT /admin/settings\"\n  \"DELETE /admin/users/5\"\n  \"GET /admin/logs\"\n  \"GET /admin/reports/export\"\n  \"POST /admin/backup\"\n)\n\nfor entry in \"${ADMIN_ENDPOINTS[@]}\"; do\n  method=$(echo \"$entry\" | cut -d' ' -f1)\n  endpoint=$(echo \"$entry\" | cut -d' ' -f2)\n  echo -n \"$method $endpoint (as user): \"\n  status=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n    -X \"$method\" \\\n    -H \"Authorization: $USER_TOKEN\" \\\n    -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n    \"https://target.example.com$endpoint\")\n  if [ \"$status\" == \"200\" ] || [ \"$status\" == \"201\" ]; then\n    echo \"VULNERABLE ($status)\"\n  else\n    echo \"OK ($status)\"\n  fi\ndone\n\n# Test with method override headers\ncurl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n  -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: $USER_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"X-HTTP-Method-Override: DELETE\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/admin/users/5\"\n\n# Test with different HTTP methods\nfor method in GET POST PUT PATCH DELETE OPTIONS HEAD; do\n  echo -n \"$method /admin/users: \"\n  curl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n    -X \"$method\" \\\n    -H \"Authorization: $USER_TOKEN\" \\\n    \"https://target.example.com/admin/users\"\n  echo\ndone\n```\n\n### Step 4: Test Horizontal Privilege Escalation\n\nVerify that users cannot access resources belonging to other users at the same privilege level.\n\n```bash\n# User A (ID: 101) testing access to User B's (ID: 102) \"https://target.example.com/api/users/102/documents/1\" -w \"%{http_code}\"\n```\n\n### Step 5: Test Function-Level Access Control\n\nVerify that specific functions enforce authorization higher-privilege functionality (user -> admin) |\n| **Horizontal Privilege Escalation** | User accessing another user's resources at the same privilege level |\n| **Function-Level Access Control** | Authorization checks on specific features/functions regardless of URL |\n| **RBAC** | Role-Based Access Control - permissions assigned to roles, roles assigned to users |\n| **ABAC** | Attribute-Based Access Control - permissions based on user/resource/environment attributes |\n| **Multi-Tenant Isolation** | Ensuring data and functionality separation between different organizations/tenants |\n| **Insecure Direct Object Reference** | Accessing objects by manipulating identifiers without authorization checks |\n| **Missing Function-Level Check** | Endpoint exists but does not verify the caller has permission to invoke it |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| **Burp Suite Professional** | Request interception and role-based testing |\n| **Authorize (Burp Extension)** | Automated access control testing across sessions |\n| **AutoRepeater (Burp Extension)** | Automatically replays requests with different auth contexts |\n| **Postman** | API testing with environment switching between roles |\n| **ffuf** | Discovering hidden endpoints that may lack access controls |\n| **OWASP ZAP** | Access control testing with context-aware scanning |\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario 1: Admin Panel Without Auth Check\nThe `/admin/dashboard` endpoint returns the admin panel when accessed with a regular user's session token. The front-end hides the admin menu, but the back-end does not enforce role checks.\n\n### Scenario 2: API Endpoint Missing Authorization\nThe `DELETE /api/users/{id}` endpoint checks for authentication (valid token) but not authorization (admin role). Any authenticated user can delete any other user's account.\n\n### Scenario 3: Tenant Data Leakage\nA SaaS application uses `tenant_id` in API request headers. Changing the `X-Tenant-ID` header to another tenant's ID returns their data, bypassing tenant isolation.\n\n### Scenario 4: Mass Assignment Role Escalation\nThe user profile update endpoint at `PUT /api/users/{id}` accepts a `role` field in the JSON body. Submitting `\"role\":\"admin\"` alongside a profile update elevates the user to administrator.\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Broken Access Control Assessment Report\n\n**Target**: target.example.com\n**Assessment Date**: 2024-01-15\n**OWASP Category**: A01:2021 - Broken Access Control\n\n### Access Control Matrix Results\n| Endpoint | Admin | Manager | User | Guest | Expected | Actual |\n|----------|-------|---------|------|-------|----------|--------|\n| GET /admin/dashboard | 200 | 200 | 200 | 302 | Admin only | FAIL |\n| DELETE /api/users/{id} | 200 | 200 | 200 | 401 | Admin only | FAIL |\n| GET /api/users/other/profile | 200 | 200 | 200 | 401 | Own only | FAIL |\n| PUT /api/users/other/settings | 200 | 200 | 200 | 401 | Own only | FAIL |\n| GET /api/org/other-tenant | 200 | 200 | 200 | 401 | Same tenant | FAIL |\n\n### Critical Findings\n1. **Vertical Escalation**: Regular users can access /admin/* endpoints\n2. **Horizontal IDOR**: Users can read/modify other users' profiles\n3. **Tenant Isolation**: Cross-tenant data access via header manipulation\n4. **Mass Assignment**: Role escalation via profile update endpoint\n\n### Impact\n- Complete administrative access for any authenticated user\n- Full user data access across all accounts (15,000+ users)\n- Cross-tenant data breach affecting 200+ organizations\n- Account takeover via profile modification\n\n### Recommendation\n1. URL\n     * rewriting, gets promoted to an authenticated session.\n     */\n    private boolean presentedByIdClient(HttpServletRequest request, String identifier) {\n        if * Builds and emits the cookie that carries the session identifier handed over by authentication.\n */\npublic final class SessionCookieFactory {\n\n    /** Plenty for any opaque identifier, while keeping a hostile \"provided\" value bounded. */\n    private static final int MAX_SESSION_ID_LENGTH = 512;\n\n    private SessionCookieFactory() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @param sessionId opaque identifier produced by the authentication step; it is copied into the\n     *                  cookie verbatim, so it is validated here as the boundary that guards the\n     *                  {@code Set-Cookie} header\n     */\n    public static Cookie forSession(SessionCookieConfig config, String sessionId) {\n        Objects.requireNonNull(config, \"config\");\n        Cookie cookie = new Cookie(config.effectiveName(), requireValidSessionId(sessionId));\n        cookie.setHttpOnly(config.httpOnly());\n        cookie.setSecure(config.secure());\n        cookie.setPath(config.path());\n A clustered deployment has to replace this with a shared\n * store, otherwise a session issued by one node is unknown to the next one that receives the cookie.\n */\npublic final class InMemorySessionRegistry implements SessionRegistry {\n\n    private static final Duration PURGE_INTERVAL = Duration.ofMinutes(1);\n\n    private final Map<String, Session> sessions = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final AtomicLong nextPurgeEpochMilli = new AtomicLong();\n\n    @Override\n    public void register(String sessionId, String subjectId, Duration ttl) {\n        Instant now = Instant.now();\n        purgeExpired(now);\n        Instant expiresAt = now.plus(ttl);\n        if (sessions.putIfAbsent(sessionId, new Session(sessionId, subjectId, expiresAt)) != null) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"session identifier already in use\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public Session find(String sessionId) {\n        if (sessionId == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        Session session = sessions.get(sessionId);\n        if (session == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (session.isExpiredAt(Instant.now())) {\n            sessions.remove(sessionId, session);\n            return null;\n        }\n        return session;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void remove(String sessionId) {\n        if (sessionId != null) {\n            sessions.remove(sessionId);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Expired sessions are rejected on read; this only stops a long-running node from accumulating\n     * entries for clients that never come back.\n     */\n    private void purgeExpired(Instant now) {\n        long nowMilli = now.toEpochMilli();\n        long scheduled = nextPurgeEpochMilli.get();\n        if (nowMilli < scheduled || !nextPurgeEpochMilli.compareAndSet(scheduled, nowMilli + PURGE_INTERVAL.toMillis())) {\n            return;\n        }\n        sessions.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> entry.getValue().isExpiredAt(now));\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"c9f289a6-2c37-4501-97fa-273aeed86ffe","session_id":"2b41e2df-dc0b-4f8a-8b29-967490f5bc42","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"c9f289a6-2c37-4501-97fa-273aeed86ffe","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I now have all the content and line numbers I need. If someone set `auth.cookie.sameSite=None`, the cookie would be sent cross-site, and logout CSRF (and login CSRF if attributes were client-set) would become exploitable. So the CSRF protection hinges entirely on SameSite not being None. No high/critical vulnerabilities were found.\nThere is **1 low** finding (CSRF defense-in-depth) and **2 informational** findings\n(session-cookie lifetime management; no HSTS emitted by the WAR). The remainder of the report\ndocuments the controls that were verified to be *present and correct*, plus non-vulnerability\ndeployment constraints.\n\nAll `file:line` references are relative to the project root.\n\n---\n\n## Findings\n\n### F1 — LOW — CWE-352 (Cross-Site Request Forgery)\n**No anti-CSRF token and no Origin/Referer validation; CSRF protection rests entirely on the\nsession cookie's `SameSite` attribute plus POST-only handlers.**\n\n- `src/main/java/com/example/auth/web/LogoutServlet.java:54-64` — the state change\n  (`sessions.remove(identifier)`, `:58`) performs no CSRF token, `Origin`, or `Referer` check.\n- `src/main/java/com/example/auth/web/LoginServlet.java:78-103` — the login POST is likewise\n  unprotected by a token, though see the mitigation below.\n\n**Why it matters:** A cross-site attacker who can trick a victim's browser into submitting a\n`POST /api/logout` form could force the victim's session to be revoked (annoyance / log-out\nDoS). Whether that request actually carries the session cookie depends solely on\n`SameSite`. The shipped config uses `Strict` (`src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:24-26`), so the\ncookie is *not* sent cross-site and the attack is currently blocked; both endpoints also return\n`405` on `GET` (`LoginServlet.java:72-74`, `LogoutServlet.java:49-51`), which blocks the trivial\n`<img>`-tag trigger.\n\n**Residual risk:** `SessionCookieConfig` *permits* `SameSite=None` (it only validates that\n`None` is paired with `Secure`, `src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieConfig.java:63-64`).\nIf an operator ever sets `auth.cookie.sameSite=None`, the cookie is sent on cross-site requests\nand logout CSRF becomes exploitable — the design has no independent CSRF control to fall back on.\n\n**Mitigations already present:** `SameSite=Strict` in `web.xml`; `405` on `GET`; and login\nitself is CSRF-resistant *by design* because identity is read from server-set request attributes,\nnever from client input (`LoginServlet.java:79-86`) — a cross-site form POST has no attributes and\nfails closed with `401`.\n\n**Recommendation:** Add an independent CSRF control on state-changing POSTs — validate\n`Origin`/`Referer` or the `Sec-Fetch-Site` header (reject `cross-site`), or issue a per-session\nanti-CSRF token. Additionally, either forbid `SameSite=None` for this app or document that doing\nso re-enables CSRF.\n\n---\n\n### F2 — Informational — CWE-613 (Insufficient Session Expiration)\n**The client cookie is a browser-session cookie (no `Max-Age`/`Expires`) while the server-side\nsession has a 1800 s TTL; there is no idle/sliding expiration.**\n\n- `src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieConfig.java:41-42` — `DEFAULTS` uses\n  `maxAgeSeconds = -1`; `:60-61` explicitly allows `-1` (\"a cookie that dies with the browser\").\n- `src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:28-30` — `auth.session.ttlSeconds = 1800`; `web.xml` does not\n  set a cookie `Max-Age`, so the `-1` default applies.\n- `src/main/java/com/example/auth/web/LoginServlet.java:63-66` — the guard only rejects the\n  `Max-Age >= 0` case that outlives the server TTL; the `-1` case is intentionally allowed.\n\n**Why it matters (low impact):** After 1800 s the server discards the session, but the browser\nkeeps the cookie until it is closed. This is **fail-closed, not a confidentiality defect**: the\nserver is authoritative and rejects expired identifiers on every lookup\n(`src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/InMemorySessionRegistry.java:30-44`), so a stale cookie\ngrants nothing. The practical consequence is a cookie-lifetime-management / usability note (a\nlong-idle user is hard-logged-out at the TTL with no sliding extension), not an exploitable flaw.\n\n**Recommendation (optional):** If a long-lived-but-active session is desired, add idle/sliding\nexpiration; otherwise leave as-is and document that the server TTL is authoritative.\n\n---\n\n### F3 — Informational — CWE-319 (Cleartext Transmission of Sensitive Information)\n**No `Strict-Transport-Security` header is emitted by the WAR; HTTPS enforcement is delegated to\nan edge / TLS terminator.**\n\n- `src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:14-15` — comment assumes HTTPS is enforced by the deployment.\n- `src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:39-40` and `src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieFactory.java:28-31`\n  — the `Secure` + `HttpOnly` cookie flags are the primary control.\n\n**Why it matters (low impact):** If the app is ever directly reachable over plain HTTP, the\n`Secure`/`__Host-` cookie simply is not sent (fail-closed), but the *first* pre-HSTS contact is\nexposed to SSL-stripping / downgrade. HSTS is not set here because it is a deployment/edge\nconcern, but the WAR does not emit it either.\n\n**Recommendation:** Ensure the load balancer / reverse proxy sets HSTS\n(`max-age` + `includeSubDomains`, ideally `preload`); for Tomcat this is the\n`StrictTransportSecurityHeaderValve` plus `RemoteIpValve` behind TLS.\n\n---\n\n## Non-vulnerability deployment constraints (not scored)\n\n- **Single-node, in-memory session store** — `src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/InMemorySessionRegistry.java:12,20`.\n  Sessions are lost on process restart and are not shared across a cluster. Documented in the\n  README; this is an availability/usability constraint, not a confidentiality flaw. (CWE-613-adjacent.)\n- **`SameSite` emitted via a Servlet 6.0 extension attribute** — `src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieFactory.java:36,58`\n  uses `Cookie.setAttribute(\"SameSite\", ...)`, which requires a Servlet 6.0 container\n  (Tomcat 10.1+ / Jetty 11+ / WildFly 27+, per `pom.xml:21-26`). A container that drops the\n  extension attribute would silently downgrade CSRF protection to the browser default (`Lax`).\n  Pin/test the target container. (CWE-352-adjacent.)\n- **Container `JSESSIONID` SameSite is out of `web.xml`** — `src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:32-35`\n  notes Tomcat renders the *container* session cookie's `SameSite` via `context.xml`\n  (`Rfc6265CookieProcessor`), which this WAR does not control. The `JSESSIONID` `SameSite=Strict`\n  hardening therefore depends on the deployment's `context.xml`, not this artifact.\n\n---\n\n## Controls verified to be present and correct (no finding)\n\n- **Unpredictable, claim-free session id** — 256 bits from the platform CSPRNG\n  (`src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionIdentifiers.java:17-18`), encoded\n  `base64url` without padding (`:23-27`). CWE-330 mitigated.\n- **`Set-Cookie` header injection prevented** — `requireValidSessionId` bounds length (512) and\n  whitelists exactly the RFC 6265 `cookie-value` octets\n  (`src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieFactory.java:82-96,103-110`), so an\n  attacker-influenced identifier cannot break out of the cookie and append response headers.\n  CWE-113 / CWE-444 mitigated.\n- **Cookie hardening** — `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `Path=/`, host-only (no `Domain`), `__Host-`\n  prefix, and `SameSite` are all set on issue and on clear\n  (`src/main/java/com/example/auth/session/SessionCookieFactory.java:26-36`); `__Host-` is only\n  valid with no `Domain` and `Path=/` (`SessionCookieConfig.java:70-76`). CWE-1004 / CWE-1021 mitigated.\n- **Config cannot weaken `Secure`/`HttpOnly`** — any `SameSite` requires `Secure`\n  (`SessionCookieConfig.java:63-68`); the `WebInfrastructure` fallback hardcodes\n  `secure=true`/`httpOnly=true`, so context params can only *narrow* the cookie.\n- **Session-fixation defense** — the identifier is read only from server-set request attributes\n  (fail-closed `401`, `LoginServlet.java:79-86`); an identifier the client already presented is\n  refused (`LoginServlet.java:88-91,111-116`); a URL-derived identifier is refused\n  (`LoginServlet.java:112-113`); the pre-auth `HttpSession` is discarded (`LoginServlet.java:93`).\n  CWE-384 mitigated.\n- **Logout revokes the server-side record before expiring the cookie** —\n  `src/main/java/com/example/auth/web/LogoutServlet.java:56-58` (remove first) then `:64`\n  (clear). A copy of the id stolen before logout stops working.\n- **POST-only endpoints** — both handlers return `405` + `Allow: POST` on `GET`\n  (`LoginServlet.java:72-74`, `LogoutServlet.java:49-51`), preventing GET-based CSRF and\n  log-out-via-`<img>` DoS.\n- **No identifier in URLs / `Referer`** — `<tracking-mode>COOKIE</tracking-mode>` only\n  (`src/main/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:44`).\n- **No response caching** — `Cache-Control: no-store` on all responses (`AuthResponses.noStore`).\n- **Absence of classic sink classes** — no SQL, no unsafe deserialization, no OS command\n  execution, no hardcoded secrets/keys, and no weak or legacy cryptographic primitives anywhere\n  in `src/main` or `src/test` (verified by targeted searches across the tree).\n\n---\n\n## Skills applied\n\n- `testing-api-authentication-weaknesses`\n- `performing-security-headers-audit`\n- `performing-cryptographic-audit-of-application`\n- `performing-csrf-attack-simulation`\n- `testing-for-broken-access-control`\n- `implementing-secret-scanning-with-gitleaks` (considered; targeted search for keys/credentials/secrets — none found)\n- `exploiting-insecure-deserialization` (considered; no deserialization sinks in scope — N/A)\n\n## Note on behaviors with no matching library skill\n\nSession-fixation specifics and secure session-cookie attribute enforcement have **no dedicated\nlibrary skill** in this library.
